# The harvest contract: kwp

Generated from `profiles/kwp/extraction_schema.json` by `scripts/build_docs.py`; that file is itself generated from the profile's `extraction_spec.json` by `docpipe/extraction/schema.py`. Edit neither: change the spec and regenerate.

## Reading the contract below

The parameters, their axes, the closed lists, the questions and the
`kg` blocks below are generated by `docpipe/extraction/schema.py` off
this profile's `extraction_spec.json`, never off a hand-written
description of it (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:11` to `27`). This
profile's spec states six parameters: `energy_consumption`,
`emission`, `planning_organisation`, `heat_load`, `share` and
`building_count`, five of them numeric and sharing twelve axes
(`technology` on all five, `whole` in place of `aggregation` on `share`),
`planning_organisation` one, `role`
(`profiles/kwp/extraction_spec.json`). [The kwp
profile](../profiles/kwp.md) states the same parameters and axes in
prose; [extraction](../stages/extraction.md) fills every key below as
a document is read.

`parameter_state`, `refusal` and `summary` are the three record kinds
every profile's contract shares. `parameter_state` closes one parameter
for one document: its `state` and how many `tuple` and `refusal` lines
it produced (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:572` to `596`). `refusal`
records a claim the run did not accept: why it failed, the claim as
returned, and its source (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:493` to `524`).
`summary`, the file's last line, counts a document's tuples and
refusals by trust level and by reason
(`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:525` to `571`). The remaining `##`
sections, one per parameter, are the shapes a `tuple` line can take,
named `tuple_<uri>` and shown here after the uri.

A tuple section opens with how its row becomes a node of MHPKG
(`profiles/kwp/kg.py:2`, `:36`), then lists one `###` heading per key,
sorted alphabetically rather than grouped by coordinate
(`scripts/build_docs.py`, where a section's properties are walked in `sorted` order). Eleven belong to the `parameter`
coordinate and to every axis: the value, plus ten keys that make it
checkable without the run that produced it: state, raw wording, a flag
for a wording naming no class, a wording merely noticed and never
chosen, quote, source and window, for a year read as a base year or a target year
the row's own passage (`_link_quote`, `_link_source`), and `_producer`, the
position in the stamp's `producers` list of the pass that wrote the
coordinate after the harvest: a top-up that read it again, or the pass that
appended its row (`--top-up-parameters`); the harvest writes none, so a
missing key means the harvest read it (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:212` to
`293`). The rest belong to the row itself: `kind`, `quote`,
`tier`, `flags`, `provenance`, `at` (the address of the value's place: passage,
line, cell), `head` (the words over its table column), `drawn` (a figure's value),
`computed`, `compute`, `unit` and `unit_raw` (empty for a wording); only the value additionally carries `value_target` for a numeric parameter
whose unit chose an entry (a printed unit that is no entry leaves `unit` and
`value_target` empty and flags `unmapped:unit:<print>`), `reading` for a number
read off a figure in place of a quote, or `value_raw` and `value_uri` for a
wording (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:313` to `384`).

Two keys on a coordinate say more than its plain description does.
`x-question` is the sentence, in German, the model was actually asked
for that coordinate, so a reader can check the wording without opening
the profile's prompt; it sits on every axis and on the `parameter`
coordinate, never on the numeric `value` itself, since which number to
read is settled by the row request, not a question of its own
(`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:170`, `:401`). `x-options` appears only
where a coordinate answers from a closed list: every class it may
resolve to, with its ontology uri, its definition and the corpus
spellings recorded for it, collapsed so a long list does not crowd the
page, matching the spec both were built from.
`x-kg` is the same spec `kg` block on two different objects: an axis
that mints its own edge (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:412`), or the
row itself rather than the `parameter` coordinate
(`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:24` to `27`, `:419`).

A tuple's `provenance` names where its `quote` sits: `document_id`,
`owner_kind` and `owner_id` are always present; `page`,
`section_number`, `section_title`, `title`, `parent_section`,
`block_id`, `image`, `rects` and `via` are filled in as the
source allows (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:444` to `492`). A
coordinate's `<name>_source` and a refusal's `owner` are the shorter
`[owner_kind, owner_id]` pair instead
(`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:147` to `151`).

A tuple section closes with the `allOf` rule: a coordinate is `null`
unless its own `<name>_state` says `read` or `derived`, one branch of
the schema's `allOf` array per coordinate (`docpipe/extraction/
schema.py:296` to `310`, `:420` to `421`). A coordinate never asked and one asked and
answered `null` differ by type, not merely by convention.

The stamp and the trace close the page, describing the run rather than
one row. The stamp is `<document>.stamp.json`: whole-run keys (`spec`,
`model`, `anchors`, `page_text_transcribed`, `extraction/*`,
`review/*`, and the three that place the harvest: `docpipe` (the
version), `document` (the sha256 and size of the PDF it was read from)
and `producers` (who wrote into it)) beside the `parameter/`, `value/`,
`axis/*/*`, `slot/parameter` and `question_text/` families, written one
per question instead (`docpipe/extraction/schema.py:603` to `765`).
The owner decided on 2026-09-10 that a stamp rests on the KG/ontology
parameters alone: `stale` compares the `parameter/`, `value/`,
`axis/` and `slot/` families (`QUESTION_KEYS`,
`docpipe/extraction/runner.py:4372`), and the whole-file sha `spec`
only for a stamp that carries none of them (`COARSE`, `:4366`).
`model`, `anchors`, every `extraction/*` prompt, `page_text_transcribed`,
`review/*`, `question_text/*`, `docpipe` and `producers` are written into
the stamp so a reader can place a harvest, and are never compared.
A stamp of an older release also carries the key `extraction/harvest`; the
schema accepts it without requiring it, and a document whose stamp holds it
stays current.
`document` is the one key beside the ontology's that is compared: where the
stamp and the database both name the sha256 of the PDF and the two differ,
the document is stale like a changed question (see [the extraction
stage](../stages/extraction.md) for when that can happen). The
`value/` key counts the classes of a list and their spellings and not
their definitions, because the rows request shows the model no more.
A moved `parameter/`, `value/`, `axis/` or `slot/` key, or a moved PDF,
makes a
document eligible for `--force-stale`'s full re-harvest (`stale`,
`docpipe/extraction/runner.py:4404` to `4449`, `already_done`, `:4494`
to `4533`); redoing only the changed question is
`top_up_file`'s job (`docpipe/extraction/topup.py:420` to `538`), and
reading a parameter the spec has gained, without reading the document again,
`append_document`'s (`docpipe/extraction/topup_parameter.py:336` to `462`),
which `already_done` names in a second warning for such a document. The
trace is `<document>.trace.jsonl.gz`: its event kinds are told apart by
`t` (`schema.trace_schema`). An event that reports a model request carries
the request as it was sent (`input`) and what came back (`output`), and a
text the requests repeat is a `piece` they refer to. It is read by
`scripts/trace_report.py` and, for a cost report, by `trace_costs` in
`scripts/harvest_compare.py`, never by a resume.

One JSON object per line of a harvest file. Every line is one of the kinds below and nothing else, and each of them is closed (`additionalProperties: false`). A new record kind costs a branch in `docpipe/extraction/schema.py`, a regeneration of both checked-in schemas, and a branch in `read_harvest` in `scripts/harvest_compare.py`.

## `parameter_state`

What one parameter of the spec came to in this document. Every other state in this file belongs to a row, so a parameter that produced no row left no byte at all and "the document does not carry it" and "it was never asked" were the same empty file.

### `document_id`

### `kind`

### `parameter`

The spec's uri for the parameter, the same key a tuple carries.

### `refusals`

### `state`

### `tuples`

## `refusal`

### `claim`

The claim as the model returned it. A sentinel carries _harvest_failed with _why: the server was gone, it answered 429 or 5xx, the model answered nothing, or the reply did not fit and there was nothing left to split. A hole of a reading also carries _parameter, the parameter the passage was to be read for.

### `kind`

### `owner`

[owner_kind, owner_id]

### `parameter`

The spec parameter, or whatever the claim named.

### `reason`

Why the claim was refused; one of the reason families of verify.py and pipeline.py.

## `summary`

The last line of the file: how this document's own values are distributed. A contested identity is decided by the serializer and a second reading is a later pass, so neither is counted here. The levels are a floor, and the graph side recomputes them. A pass that appended a parameter built this line again over every tuple and refusal the file holds; the refusals of the first pass are counted as they were stored and are not revisited, so a value refused then for want of that parameter stays in the count.

### `document_id`

### `image_origin`

Values read out of a table or figure image rather than the document's text.

### `kind`

### `levels`

How many values reached each level.

### `reasons`

Why values are not an A, counted. A closed list: a reason nobody can enumerate is a reason nobody can count.

### `refusals`

### `tuples`

### `unlisted_units`

The units numbers were read with that are no entry of their parameter's list, as the plan prints them, counted: read and not converted, until the list grows and a top-up asks them again (owner decision 2026-11-07). Absent in a file written before.

## `building_count`

How the row becomes a node:

- `class_from`: `quantity`
- `node`: `value`
- `number`: `{'datatype': 'xsd:float', 'from': 'value_target', 'label': 'has number', 'schema_slot': 'has_number'}`
- `unit`: `{'from': 'unit_target', 'label': 'has unit', 'schema_slot': 'has_unit'}`

### `aggregation`

Axis 'aggregation' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless aggregation_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Wie fasst diese Zahl Werte über Zeit oder Raum zusammen?

<details><summary>What it may answer (5)</summary>

- **arithmetic mean** → `OEO_00140071` — Durchschnittswert, berechnet als arithmetisches Mittel über Datensätze.
  - also written: Mittelwert, Durchschnitt
- **instantaneous** → `OEO_00140069` — Wert, der exakt zu einem zeitlichen Nullpunkt gemessen oder modelliert wird.
  - also written: Momentanwert zu einem Zeitpunkt
- **integral** → `OEO_00140070` — Summe oder Integral von Werten innerhalb eines Zeitraums oder Gebiets, etwa eine Jahressumme, der Normalfall.
  - also written: Summe oder Integral über einen Zeitraum oder ein Gebiet, Jahreswert, Jahressumme
- **maximum** → `OEO_00140073` — Höchster Wert innerhalb einer Menge von Datenwerten in einem Bereich.
  - also written: Maximum, Spitzenwert, Spitzenlast
- **minimum** → `OEO_00140072` — Niedrigster Wert innerhalb einer Menge von Datenwerten in einem Bereich.
  - also written: Minimum, Tiefstwert

</details>

- `label`: `has aggregation type`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `has_aggregation_type`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is a named individual of OEO_00140068 aggregation type. Derived from the unit, never asked: a number of buildings is a stock at one point in time.

</details>

### `aggregation_link_quote`

Only with window `base_year` or `target_year`: the passage where the row names that state ('aggregation_raw'). 'aggregation_quote' is then the frame's passage that prints the number.

### `aggregation_link_source`

Which source 'aggregation_link_quote' was found in.

### `aggregation_means`

The entries of the document's list of names (its scenarios, its works) 'aggregation' means: the entry its name is, or the entry of each member where its words name a group; empty where they name a group the field takes no members for, or where the request found they name no such thing (owner decision 2026-11-09). The graph writes the row to each of them, an empty list to none.

### `aggregation_producer`

Who re-read 'aggregation': the position of its entry in the `producers` list of the stamp beside the harvest. Written by a top-up for each coordinate it re-read, and by a pass that appended a parameter for each coordinate of the rows it wrote, never by the harvest, so a missing key means the harvest read it. A position the stamp has no entry for (the stamps were deleted) means nobody can say. Recorded and never compared.

### `aggregation_quote`

The verbatim passage carrying 'aggregation'. Present exactly when aggregation_state is 'read'.

### `aggregation_raw`

The document's own wording 'aggregation' was read from. This is what a later re-mapping onto a changed vocabulary works on, so a choice without it is counted (raw_missing).

### `aggregation_raw_foreign`

The wording does not name the option chosen for 'aggregation': the model mapped a word the spec does not list onto this class. Kept and counted, not refused: some of those mappings are right.

### `aggregation_seen`

A wording the model noticed for 'aggregation' while answering 'not stated', or offered as an answer the closed list does not hold. Vocabulary review material, never evidence.

### `aggregation_source`

Which source the passage for 'aggregation' was found in. How far that may be from the row is the axis' own rule (own | local | any).

### `aggregation_state`

How the coordinate 'aggregation' ended. Always present: a missing key and a refused reading must not look alike.

### `aggregation_window`

[stage, index] of the window 'aggregation' was read in. `own`: at the value's own place, its row with its table's title and head or its sentence with the sentences beside it. `near`: in the pieces right before and after it. `found`: in the pieces a search found for the sentence of how the plan would print it. `piece`: a work read once for the value's piece, the line that names it or shows the piece is this publication's own. `entry`: the work of the entry of the document's list the value's name is. `plan_years`: a year none of these found, read from the plan's base and target years in a request of its own. The other stages are those of harvests made before the reading in pieces: `frame` is the one that is not a window: the coordinate was read ONCE for the document and applied to this row, and the index is which of the document's pairs it came from. `base_year`: the row's passage names the document's base state by word, and the index is the pair whose quote prints the number. `target_year`: the same for the document's target. `table`: read once for the row's table and taken by this row. `head`: the year the head of the row's column prints, one of the document's pairs, taken off the table and not asked; the index is the column. `retrieval`, `rest` and `places` are the windows of the reading by passages.

### `at`

Where the value stands (owner decisions 2026-11-08): its passage, the number of its line there, the cell of a table row, and the last line of a value over several lines. The quote is that line.

### `carrier`

Axis 'carrier' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless carrier_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Für welchen Energieträger gilt diese Zahl, oder, wo ihre Tabelle oder ihr Satz nicht nach Energieträgern aufteilt, wonach teilt die Stelle stattdessen auf?

  - also written: Klärschlamm, EBS

  - also written: unvermeidbare Abwärme

It consists mostly of organic compounds obtained by the fractional distillation of petroleum, enhanced with a variety of additives.
- **Bio-Ottokraftstoff** → `OEO_00000075` — Biogasoline is a portion of matter that has a liquid state of matter and has a gasoline fuel role. It consists of bioethanol, biomethanol and products of these two substances. It is made from vegetable or animal material and thus has a biogenic origin.

Includes motor gasoline blending components (excluding additives/oxygenates), e.g. alkylates, isomerate, reformate, cracked gasoline destined for use as finished motor gasoline.
- **Ottokraftstoff** → `OEO_00010241` — A gasoline fuel is a combustion fuel that has a gasoline fuel role.
- **Ozean** → `OEO_00010105` — The ocean is a water body that consists of salt water and is covering approximately 71% of Earth's surface.
- **Petrolkoks** → `OEO_00010327` — Petroleum coke is a portion of matter with a solid state of matter that is produced in a mineral oil refining process. It consists mainly of carbon and can be uses as a fuel.
- **Photon** → `OEO_00230021` — A photon is a material entity that is a light particle.
- **Plutonium** → `OEO_00140091` — Plutonium is a portion of matter with the chemical formula Pu. It has a solid normal state of matter.
- **Primärenergieträger** → `OEO_00140078` — A primary energy carrier is an energy carrier that has the disposition primary energy carrier disposition.
- **PtL-Kraftstoff** → `OEO_00010019` — PtL fuel is a liquid synthetic fuel produced in a power-to-liquid system from water and carbon dioxide using electrical energy.
- **Raffineriegas** → `OEO_00010326` — Refinery gas is a gas mixture that is produced as a by-product of a mineral oil refining process. It mainly consists of hydrogen, methane, ethane and olefins and can be used as a fuel.
- **Rohöl** → `OEO_00000115` — Crude oil is a portion of mater of fossil origin comprising a mixture of hydrocarbons and associated impurities, such as sulphur. It exists in the liquid phase under normal surface temperature and pressure and its physical characteristics (density, viscosity, etc.) are highly variable. This category includes field or lease condensate recovered from associated and non-associated gas where it is commingled with the commercial crude oil stream.
- **Sekundärenergieträger** → `OEO_00140079` — A secondary energy carrier is an energy carrier that has the secondary energy carrier disposition.
- **Siedlungsabfälle** → `OEO_00000290` — Siedlungsabfälle: Abfallbrennstoff aus Haushalten oder nicht-industriellen gewerblichen Tätigkeiten.
  - also written: Abfall, thermische Abfallverwertung
- **Solarthermie** → `OEO_00000388` — Solarthermie: Wärmeenergie, die als Ergebnis einer solarthermischen Energieumwandlung entsteht.
  - also written: solare Wärme
- **Sonstige** → `out:other` — Etwas, das keiner der übrigen Einträge dieser Liste ist.
  - also written: Andere, sonstige Energieträger, Sonstige Wärmequellen, Rest
- **Stadtgas** → `OEO_00000186` — Gasworks gas is manufactured coal based gas that covers all types of gases produced in public utility or private plants, whose main purpose is manufacture, transport and distribution of gas. It includes gas produced by carbonisation (including gas produced by coke ovens and transferred to gasworks gas), by total gasification with or without enrichment with oil products (LPG, residual fuel oil, etc.), and by reforming and simple mixing of gases and/or air, reported under the rows ‘from other sources’. Under the transformation sector identify amounts of gasworks gas transferred to blended natural gas which will be distributed and consumed through the natural gas grid.

The production of other coal gases (i.e. coke oven gas, blast furnace gas and oxygen steel furnace gas) should be reported in the columns concerning such gases, and not as production of gasworks gas. The coal gases transferred to gasworks plants should then be reported (in their own column) in the transformation sector in the gasworks plants row. The total amount of gasworks gas resulting from transfers of other coal gases should appear in the production line for gasworks gas.
- **Steinkohle** → `OEO_00000204` — Steinkohle: Kohle mit einem Brennwert über 23 865 kJ/kg und einer mittleren Vitrinitreflexion von mindestens 0,6.
- **Strom** → `OEO_00000139` — Elektrische Energie aus geladenen Teilchen, unterscheidet sich als nicht-materieller Energieträger.
  - also written: Elektrizität, Heizungsstrom, Wärmestrom, Direktstrom, E-Wärme
- **Summe** → `out:total` — Die Zahl gilt für alle Energieträger zusammen: ihre Zeile oder Spalte summiert ausdrücklich über die Energieträger, oder ihre eigene Tabelle oder ihr Satz ist gar nicht nach Energieträgern aufgeteilt, nur nach anderem (Gebäudetyp, Sektor, Gebiet). Der Wärmebedarf der Gebäude eines Gebiets bleibt Summe, solange die Stelle nicht nach Energieträgern aufteilt.
  - also written: Gesamt, Gesamtwert, Gesamtsumme, alle Energieträger, insgesamt

- **Thorium** → `OEO_00140092` — Thorium is a portion of matter with the chemical formula Th. It has a solid normal state of matter.
- **Torf** → `OEO_00000320` — Peat is a or portion of matter that is a soft, porous or compressed, sedimentary deposit of plant origin with high water content (up to 90 % in the raw state) and can be used as combustion fuel. As the natural formation of peat takes at least centuries, peat is usually considered having a fossil origin.

- **Wasserstoff** → `OEO_00000220` — Wasserstoff: Stoff mit der chemischen Formel H2, der gasförmig vorliegt und als Brennstoff verwendet werden kann.

- **Wasserstoff aus Dampfreformierung** → `OEO_00010381` — Steam reforming hydrogen is hydrogen that is a physical output of a steam reforming process.

- **Wasserstoff aus Methanpyrolyse** → `OEO_00020513` — Methane pyrolytic hydrogen is synthetic hydrogen that is produced from pyrolysis of methane and solid CO2 storage.

- **fester erneuerbarer Brennstoff** → `OEO_00010149` — A solid renewable fuel is a renewable fuel that has a solid state of matter under normal conditions.
- **fester fossiler Brennstoff** → `OEO_00000391` — A solid fossil fuel is a fossil fuel that has a solid state of matter under normal conditions.

- **fossiler Dieselkraftstoff** → `OEO_00000131` — Diesel fuel is gas diesel oil used on-road for diesel compression ignition (cars, trucks, etc.), usually of low sulphur content.

- `label`: `refers to energy carrier`
- `outside_root`: `{'OEO_00000056': 'ambient heat', 'OEO_00000132': 'district heating', 'OEO_00000139': 'electrical energy', 'OEO_00000191': 'geothermal energy', 'OEO_00000388': 'solar thermal energy', 'OEO_00010114': 'waste heat', 'OEO_00140104': 'surface water heat', 'OEO_00140105': 'waste water heat', 'OEO_00310004': 'industrial waste heat'}`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `refers_to_energy_carrier`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is an OEO class (punning). The predicate is the MHPKG schema's slot, read from mhpkg_schema.yaml at serialization: refers to energy carrier. Its range is energy carrier, and the classes in outside_root are not under it in OEO; they are written all the same and counted, as input for the carrier axioms the ontology side is adding.

</details>

### `carrier_link_quote`

Only with window `base_year` or `target_year`: the passage where the row names that state ('carrier_raw'). 'carrier_quote' is then the frame's passage that prints the number.

### `carrier_link_source`

Which source 'carrier_link_quote' was found in.

### `carrier_means`

The entries of the document's list of names (its scenarios, its works) 'carrier' means: the entry its name is, or the entry of each member where its words name a group; empty where they name a group the field takes no members for, or where the request found they name no such thing (owner decision 2026-11-09). The graph writes the row to each of them, an empty list to none.

### `carrier_producer`

Who re-read 'carrier': the position of its entry in the `producers` list of the stamp beside the harvest. Written by a top-up for each coordinate it re-read, and by a pass that appended a parameter for each coordinate of the rows it wrote, never by the harvest, so a missing key means the harvest read it. A position the stamp has no entry for (the stamps were deleted) means nobody can say. Recorded and never compared.

### `carrier_quote`

The verbatim passage carrying 'carrier'. Present exactly when carrier_state is 'read'.

### `carrier_raw`

The document's own wording 'carrier' was read from. This is what a later re-mapping onto a changed vocabulary works on, so a choice without it is counted (raw_missing).

### `carrier_raw_foreign`

The wording does not name the option chosen for 'carrier': the model mapped a word the spec does not list onto this class. Kept and counted, not refused: some of those mappings are right.

### `carrier_seen`

A wording the model noticed for 'carrier' while answering 'not stated', or offered as an answer the closed list does not hold. Vocabulary review material, never evidence.

### `carrier_source`

Which source the passage for 'carrier' was found in. How far that may be from the row is the axis' own rule (own | local | any).

### `carrier_state`

How the coordinate 'carrier' ended. Always present: a missing key and a refused reading must not look alike.

### `carrier_window`

[stage, index] of the window 'carrier' was read in. `own`: at the value's own place, its row with its table's title and head or its sentence with the sentences beside it. `near`: in the pieces right before and after it. `found`: in the pieces a search found for the sentence of how the plan would print it. `piece`: a work read once for the value's piece, the line that names it or shows the piece is this publication's own. `entry`: the work of the entry of the document's list the value's name is. `plan_years`: a year none of these found, read from the plan's base and target years in a request of its own. The other stages are those of harvests made before the reading in pieces: `frame` is the one that is not a window: the coordinate was read ONCE for the document and applied to this row, and the index is which of the document's pairs it came from. `base_year`: the row's passage names the document's base state by word, and the index is the pair whose quote prints the number. `target_year`: the same for the document's target. `table`: read once for the row's table and taken by this row. `head`: the year the head of the row's column prints, one of the document's pairs, taken off the table and not asked; the index is the column. `retrieval`, `rest` and `places` are the windows of the reading by passages.

### `catalogue`

The open catalogue record a value was looked up in, not read off the page: its source and id, what was searched, what it holds and which of its values matched the document's. The quote is the read value it was looked up with; the flag catalogue goes with it.

### `compute`

The sandbox runs behind a computed value: {code, stdout, ok, error}, the last with the numbers it was worked out from (inputs) and what they make (expected).

### `computed`

The value came out of the sandbox, not off the page; the quote proves the inputs it was computed from.

### `drawn`

A figure's value only: whether its image only draws the number (a bar, a segment, an area, a point of a curve) rather than printing it, as the reading said. True sets the flag read_off.

### `flags`

Non-fatal verifier findings. mapped:<axis>:<wording>-><uri> is the model mapping a word the spec does not list; period:* says whether a bare amount was shown to be a yearly one; review:* is what a second reading of this value under a narrower window came to, and only review:disagree is a reason.

### `head`

The head of the table column the number stands in, as the plan prints it. Taken from the table, never asked. Absent where the number is no table cell or its column is not certain.

### `parameter`

Spec parameter: Gebäudeanzahl.

The prompt's own wording:

> Um WELCHE Kennzahl handelt es sich bei dieser Zahl? Entscheide danach, was die Quelle über sie sagt: die Einheit, die Spalten- oder Zeilenbeschriftung und die Tabellen- oder Abschnittsüberschrift. Eine Angabe in t CO2 ist eine Emission, eine Angabe in MWh oder GWh ein Energieverbrauch. Zitiere die Stelle, aus der das hervorgeht. Eine Angabe in kW, MW oder GW ist eine Leistung. Eine Angabe in % ist ein Anteil. Eine Zahl von Gebäuden ist eine Gebäudeanzahl.

### `qualifier`

Axis 'qualifier' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless qualifier_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Welches Wort direkt bei dieser Zahl sagt, wie genau sie gilt, etwa „ca.“, „bis zu“, „mehr als“ oder „zwischen“?

<details><summary>What it may answer (5)</summary>

- **höchstens** → `out:at_most` — Der Plan nennt die Zahl als Obergrenze: Der Wert ist höchstens so groß.
  - also written: bis zu, maximal, max., weniger als, unter
- **mindestens** → `out:at_least` — Der Plan nennt die Zahl als Untergrenze: Der Wert ist mindestens so groß.
  - also written: mehr als, über, ab, min.
- **oberes Ende einer Spanne** → `out:range_high` — Die größere von zwei Zahlen, zwischen denen der Wert liegt.
  - also written: bis
- **ungefähr** → `out:approximate` — Der Plan nennt die Zahl als ungefähren Wert.
  - also written: ca., circa, rund, etwa, knapp, annähernd
- **unteres Ende einer Spanne** → `out:range_low` — Die kleinere von zwei Zahlen, zwischen denen der Wert liegt.
  - also written: zwischen, von

</details>

### `quantity`

Axis 'quantity' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless quantity_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Welche Größe ist diese Zahl?

<details><summary>What it may answer (1)</summary>

- **building count** → `MHPO_00030021` — Gebäudeanzahl: Misst die Anzahl der Gebäude in einem Gebäudebestand.
  - also written: Gebäudeanzahl, Anzahl Gebäude, Anzahl der Gebäude

</details>

- `role`: `type`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

The class of the value node itself (rdf:type), derived from the unit: a number of buildings is a building count and nothing else. The MHPKG schema holds no building count yet, so the strict graph leaves it out.

</details>

### `quantity_link_quote`

Only with window `base_year` or `target_year`: the passage where the row names that state ('quantity_raw'). 'quantity_quote' is then the frame's passage that prints the number.

### `quantity_link_source`

Which source 'quantity_link_quote' was found in.

### `quantity_means`

The entries of the document's list of names (its scenarios, its works) 'quantity' means: the entry its name is, or the entry of each member where its words name a group; empty where they name a group the field takes no members for, or where the request found they name no such thing (owner decision 2026-11-09). The graph writes the row to each of them, an empty list to none.

### `quantity_producer`

Who re-read 'quantity': the position of its entry in the `producers` list of the stamp beside the harvest. Written by a top-up for each coordinate it re-read, and by a pass that appended a parameter for each coordinate of the rows it wrote, never by the harvest, so a missing key means the harvest read it. A position the stamp has no entry for (the stamps were deleted) means nobody can say. Recorded and never compared.

### `quantity_quote`

The verbatim passage carrying 'quantity'. Present exactly when quantity_state is 'read'.

### `quantity_raw`

The document's own wording 'quantity' was read from. This is what a later re-mapping onto a changed vocabulary works on, so a choice without it is counted (raw_missing).

### `quantity_raw_foreign`

The wording does not name the option chosen for 'quantity': the model mapped a word the spec does not list onto this class. Kept and counted, not refused: some of those mappings are right.

### `quantity_seen`

A wording the model noticed for 'quantity' while answering 'not stated', or offered as an answer the closed list does not hold. Vocabulary review material, never evidence.

### `quantity_source`

Which source the passage for 'quantity' was found in. How far that may be from the row is the axis' own rule (own | local | any).

### `quantity_state`

How the coordinate 'quantity' ended. Always present: a missing key and a refused reading must not look alike.

### `quantity_window`

[stage, index] of the window 'quantity' was read in. `own`: at the value's own place, its row with its table's title and head or its sentence with the sentences beside it. `near`: in the pieces right before and after it. `found`: in the pieces a search found for the sentence of how the plan would print it. `piece`: a work read once for the value's piece, the line that names it or shows the piece is this publication's own. `entry`: the work of the entry of the document's list the value's name is. `plan_years`: a year none of these found, read from the plan's base and target years in a request of its own. The other stages are those of harvests made before the reading in pieces: `frame` is the one that is not a window: the coordinate was read ONCE for the document and applied to this row, and the index is which of the document's pairs it came from. `base_year`: the row's passage names the document's base state by word, and the index is the pair whose quote prints the number. `target_year`: the same for the document's target. `table`: read once for the row's table and taken by this row. `head`: the year the head of the row's column prints, one of the document's pairs, taken off the table and not asked; the index is the column. `retrieval`, `rest` and `places` are the windows of the reading by passages.

### `quote`

The passage of the owner source that contains the value, checked by whitespace-collapsed containment.

### `range`

Both ends of the range this value is one end of, [lower, upper] as the plan prints them: written on both rows when one reads as the lower and the next as the upper end under the same quote with the same coordinates otherwise.

### `read_off`

The number stands in no text and was read off the figure's image: the reading is its evidence in place of a quote, and the flag read_off goes with it, a reason of level C (owner decisions 2026-11-05 and 2026-11-07).

### `reading`

What was read off the figure's image: the element, the number and the unit of its axis. Present only with read_off.

### `scenario`

Axis 'scenario' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless scenario_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Auf welchen Zustand des Plans bezieht sich diese Zahl, auf seinen Bestand, ein Trend- oder ein Zielszenario?

<details><summary>What it may answer (3)</summary>

- **Bestand** → `status_quo` — Erhoben oder bilanziert für ein vergangenes oder das laufende Jahr: Bestandsanalyse, Ist-Zustand, Bilanzjahr, Ausgangslage, Energiebilanz, „bislang“, „derzeit“, „wird verbraucht“, oder ein Jahr vor der Erstellung des Plans im Titel.
  - also written: Ist-Zustand, Bilanzjahr, Status quo, Bestandsanalyse, Basisjahr, Ist-Zustandsanalyse, Ist-Analyse, Erstellungsjahr
- **Trendszenario** → `trend` — Die Fortschreibung ohne zusätzliche Maßnahmen.
  - also written: Referenzszenario, Weiter-wie-bisher, Business as usual
- **Zielszenario** → `target` — Der angestrebte Zustand eines künftigen Jahres, wie der Plan ihn erreichen will, auch unter einem eigenen Namen des Plans („bei erhöhter Energieeinsparung“, „Umsetzungsszenario 2“, „Transformationspfad“).
  - also written: Zielbild, Zielwert, Zielpfad, Umsetzungsszenario, Transformationspfad

</details>

- `linked_by`: `{'label': 'has part', 'node': 'heatplan', 'schema_slot': 'has_part'}`
- `map`: `{'status_quo': {'schema_class': 'AggregatedInventoryAnalysis'}, 'target': {'schema_class': 'TargetScenario'}, 'trend': {'class': 'OEO_00020311', 'note': 'with existing measures scenario. Not OEO_00020314 reference scenario: that one is defined by a ROLE (`scenario and has role some reference role`) and carries no content, so it says a scenario was used as a comparison and not what it assumes. A Trendszenario is the continuation under the measures already decided, which is what WEM means.'}}`
- `role`: `parent`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Which container node holds the value: the plan has the container by `linked_by`, and the edge from the container down to the value is structural and named in kg.py. Only the target scenario is serialized today (G2).

</details>

### `sector`

Axis 'sector' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless sector_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Für welchen Verbrauchssektor gilt diese Zahl, oder, wo ihre Tabelle oder ihr Satz nicht nach Sektoren aufteilt, wonach teilt die Stelle stattdessen auf?

<details><summary>What it may answer (17)</summary>

- **Abfall- und Abwassersektor** → `OEO_00010036` — A waste and wastewater sector is a sector that covers activities related the collection and treatment of waste and wastewater.
- **Energienachfragesektor** → `OEO_00000128` — An energy demand sector is a sector that covers energy consumers.
- **Energieumwandlungssektor** → `OEO_00000158` — An energy transformation sector is a sector that covers energy production, energy transformation from primary to secondary energy and transport of energy to the demand sector.
- **GHD** → `OEO_00000405` — Sektor für nicht-industrielle gewerbliche Aktivitäten.
  - also written: Gewerbe, Handel, Dienstleistungen, Gewerbe/Handel/Dienstleistungen, öffentliche Gebäude, öffentliche Liegenschaften, kommunale Einrichtungen, GHD/Kommune, Gewerbe, wirtschaftlich genutzte Gebäude, kommerzieller Sektor
- **Gebäudesektor** → `OEO_00010034` — A building sector is a sector that covers buildings.
- **Industrie** → `OEO_00000227` — Sektor für industrielle Aktivitäten mit Energiewandlung.
  - also written: Industriesektor, verarbeitendes Gewerbe
- **Landwirtschaft** → `OEO_00010035` — Sektor für Landwirtschaft, Forstwirtschaft und Landnutzung.
  - also written: Land- und Forstwirtschaft
- **Mineralölverarbeitung** → `OEO_00010319` — A mineral oil refining sector is an energy transformation sector that covers mineral oil refining.
- **Private Haushalte** → `OEO_00000214` — Sektor für private Haushalte und Wohngebäude.
  - also written: Haushalte, Privathaushalte, Wohnen, Wohngebäude, Haushaltssektor, Einfamilienhaus, Mehrfamilienhaus
- **Quellgruppe** → `OEO_00010360` — A source category is a sector that is defined by a common reporting format.
- **Sonstige** → `out:other` — Etwas, das keiner der übrigen Einträge dieser Liste ist.
  - also written: Andere
- **Stromsektor** → `OEO_00000145` — An electricity sector is a transformation sector that covers electricity generation and transport of electrical energy to consumers of electrical energy.
- **Summe** → `out:total` — Die Zahl gilt für alle Verbraucher zusammen: ihre Zeile oder Spalte summiert ausdrücklich über die Sektoren, sie heißt ausdrücklich Gesamtverbrauch oder Gesamtbedarf aller Verbraucher, oder ihre eigene Tabelle oder ihr Satz ist gar nicht nach Sektoren aufgeteilt, nur nach anderem (Energieträger, Gebiet). Ein Gebiet („Gesamtstadt“, „Stadtgebiet“) und die Gesamtleistung von Anlagen sind keine Summe über Sektoren.
  - also written: Gesamt, alle Sektoren, insgesamt, Gesamtverbrauch, Gesamtbedarf
- **Teilsektor** → `OEO_00010464` — A subsector is a sector that is a part of an other sector.
- **Verkehr** → `OEO_00000422` — Sektor für den Transport von Personen und Gütern.
  - also written: Verkehrssektor
- **Wärme- und Kältesektor** → `OEO_00000213` — A heating and cooling sector is a sector that covers heating and cooling activities.
- **internationaler Verkehr** → `OEO_00010320` — An international transport sector is a transport sector of international transport processes.

</details>

- `label`: `refers to sector`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `refers_to_sector`
- `total_class`: `OEO_00000128`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is an OEO sector class. The predicate is the MHPKG schema's slot, read from mhpkg_schema.yaml at serialization. A number its table does not split by sector (out:total) is written as total_class, the parent the schema names for a figure reported without a sector breakdown.

</details>

### `spatial_scope`

Axis 'spatial_scope' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless spatial_scope_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Für welches Gebiet gilt diese Zahl, für die ganze Gemeinde oder für ein Teilgebiet mit seinem Namen?

<details><summary>What it may answer (2)</summary>

- **Gemeindegebiet** → `municipality` — Das ganze Gemeinde- oder Stadtgebiet des Plans.
  - also written: Gesamtstadt, Stadtgebiet, gesamtes Plangebiet, Gemeinde insgesamt
- **Teilgebiet** → `sub_area` — Ein benannter Teil davon, ein Quartier, ein Fokusgebiet, ein Wärmenetzgebiet, mit seinem Namen.
  - also written: Fokusgebiet, Quartier, Stadtteil, Baublock, Wärmenetzgebiet, Ortsteil

</details>

- `map`: `{'municipality': {'note': "the plan's own area, minted from the AGS"}, 'sub_area': {'class': 'MHPO_00020019', 'linked_by': {'label': 'part of', 'node': 'municipality', 'predicate': 'BFO_0000050', 'prefix': 'obo'}}}`
- `role`: `parent`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

The schema has no relation from a value to its area, so a sub_area value is serialized with the area in its IRI and its comment but without an edge to the area node. Not TERM REQUEST 1, which asks for the plan's area.

</details>

### `technology`

Axis 'technology' of Gebäudeanzahl. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless technology_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Welcher Wärmeerzeugungstechnologie ordnet der Plan diese Zahl ausdrücklich zu, oder, wo ihre Tabelle oder ihr Satz nicht nach Technologien aufteilt, wonach teilt die Stelle stattdessen auf?

<details><summary>What it may answer (12)</summary>

- **Biomasseheizung** → `OEO_00020493` — Biomasseheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Biomasse.
  - also written: Holzheizung, Pelletheizung, Hackschnitzelheizung, Biomassekessel
- **Direktelektrische Heizung** → `OEO_00020487` — Direktelektrische Heizung: Wandelt elektrische Energie direkt in Wärme mittels elektrischer Heizelemente um.
  - also written: Stromdirektheizung, Elektroheizung, Nachtspeicherheizung
- **Gasheizung** → `OEO_00020491` — Gasheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Erdgas.
  - also written: Gaskessel, Gasbrennwertkessel, Gastherme
- **Geothermie** → `OEO_00020306` — Geothermie: Kombiniert geothermische Einheiten und Wärmeprozesse zur Wärmeerzeugung.
  - also written: Geothermieanlage, Erdwärmesonden, Erdwärmekollektoren
- **Kohleheizung** → `OEO_00020489` — A coal powered heating technology is a combustion-based heat generation technology that describes how to combine combustion-based heaters, heat gain processes, coal and energy in a specific way.
- **Solarthermie** → `OEO_00020307` — Solarthermie: Nutzt Solareinheiten und solare Umwandlungsprozesse zur Wärmeerzeugung.
  - also written: Solarthermieanlage, solarthermische Anlage
- **Sonstige** → `out:other` — Etwas, das keiner der übrigen Einträge dieser Liste ist.
  - also written: Andere, sonstige Wärmeerzeuger
- **Summe** → `out:total` — Die Zahl gilt für alle Wärmeerzeuger zusammen: ihre Zeile oder Spalte summiert ausdrücklich über die Technologien, oder ihre eigene Tabelle oder ihr Satz ist gar nicht nach Heiztechniken aufgeteilt, nur nach anderem. Ein Energieträger allein („Erdgas“) ist keine Technologie, eine „Gasheizung“ schon.
  - also written: Gesamt, insgesamt, alle Technologien
- **Verbrennungsbasierte Heizung** → `OEO_00020488` — A combustion based heating technology is a heat generation technology that describes how to combine combustion-based heaters, heat gain processes, combustion fuels and energy in a specific way.
- **Wasserstoffheizung** → `OEO_00020492` — Wasserstoffheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Wasserstoff.
  - also written: Wasserstoffkessel, H2-Kessel
- **Wärmepumpe** → `OEO_00020486` — Wärmepumpe: Kombiniert Wärmepumpen, Wärmegewinnungsprozesse und Energieträger zur Wärmeerzeugung.
  - also written: Wärmepumpen, Luft-Wasser-Wärmepumpe, Sole-Wasser-Wärmepumpe, Großwärmepumpe
- **Ölheizung** → `OEO_00020490` — Ölheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Heizöl.
  - also written: Ölkessel, Heizölkessel

</details>

- `label`: `refers to technology`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `refers_to_technology`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is an OEO heat generation technology class (punning), the MHPKG schema's slot. A number not split by technology (out:total) writes no edge: the schema's slot is optional.

</details>

### `technology_link_quote`

### `technology_means`

The entries of the document's list of names (its scenarios, its works) 'technology' means: the entry its name is, or the entry of each member where its words name a group; empty where they name a group the field takes no members for, or where the request found they name no such thing (owner decision 2026-11-09). The graph writes the row to each of them, an empty list to none.

### `technology_producer`

Who re-read 'technology': the position of its entry in the `producers` list of the stamp beside the harvest. Written by a top-up for each coordinate it re-read, and by a pass that appended a parameter for each coordinate of the rows it wrote, never by the harvest, so a missing key means the harvest read it. A position the stamp has no entry for (the stamps were deleted) means nobody can say. Recorded and never compared.

### `technology_quote`

The verbatim passage carrying 'technology'. Present exactly when technology_state is 'read'.

### `technology_raw`

The document's own wording 'technology' was read from. This is what a later re-mapping onto a changed vocabulary works on, so a choice without it is counted (raw_missing).

### `technology_raw_foreign`

The wording does not name the option chosen for 'technology': the model mapped a word the spec does not list onto this class. Kept and counted, not refused: some of those mappings are right.

### `technology_seen`

A wording the model noticed for 'technology' while answering 'not stated', or offered as an answer the closed list does not hold. Vocabulary review material, never evidence.

### `technology_source`

Which source the passage for 'technology' was found in. How far that may be from the row is the axis' own rule (own | local | any).

### `technology_state`

How the coordinate 'technology' ended. Always present: a missing key and a refused reading must not look alike.

### `technology_window`

[stage, index] of the window 'technology' was read in. `own`: at the value's own place, its row with its table's title and head or its sentence with the sentences beside it. `near`: in the pieces right before and after it. `found`: in the pieces a search found for the sentence of how the plan would print it. `piece`: a work read once for the value's piece, the line that names it or shows the piece is this publication's own. `entry`: the work of the entry of the document's list the value's name is. `plan_years`: a year none of these found, read from the plan's base and target years in a request of its own. The other stages are those of harvests made before the reading in pieces: `frame` is the one that is not a window: the coordinate was read ONCE for the document and applied to this row, and the index is which of the document's pairs it came from. `base_year`: the row's passage names the document's base state by word, and the index is the pair whose quote prints the number. `target_year`: the same for the document's target. `table`: read once for the row's table and taken by this row. `head`: the year the head of the row's column prints, one of the document's pairs, taken off the table and not asked; the index is the column. `retrieval`, `rest` and `places` are the windows of the reading by passages.

### `tier`

text_located: the quote sits in the document's own refined text, so it can be checked against the PDF. visual_source: it sits in a table transcription, a caption or a figure description, which are model output and want human curation.

<details><summary>What it may answer (5)</summary>

- **arithmetic mean** → `OEO_00140071` — Durchschnittswert, berechnet als arithmetisches Mittel über Datensätze.

- **instantaneous** → `OEO_00140069` — Wert, der exakt zu einem zeitlichen Nullpunkt gemessen oder modelliert wird.
  - also written: Momentanwert zu einem Zeitpunkt
- **integral** → `OEO_00140070` — Summe oder Integral von Werten innerhalb eines Zeitraums oder Gebiets, etwa eine Jahressumme, der Normalfall.

- **maximum** → `OEO_00140073` — Höchster Wert innerhalb einer Menge von Datenwerten in einem Bereich.
  - also written: Maximum, Spitzenwert, Spitzenlast
- **minimum** → `OEO_00140072` — Niedrigster Wert innerhalb einer Menge von Datenwerten in einem Bereich.
  - also written: Minimum, Tiefstwert

</details>

The prompt's own wording:

> Für welchen Energieträger gilt diese Zahl, oder, wo ihre Tabelle oder ihr Satz nicht nach Energieträgern aufteilt, wonach teilt die Stelle stattdessen auf?

<details><summary>What it may answer (137)</summary>

- **Abfallbrennstoff** → `OEO_00000439` — Brennstoff, bei dem das Material Abfall ist, umfasst alle Abfallarten.
  - also written: Klärschlamm, EBS
- **Abwärme** → `OEO_00010114` — Abwärme: Wärmeenergie, die bei einem Energieumwandlungsprozess entsteht und ungenutzt an die Umwelt abgegeben wird.
  - also written: unvermeidbare Abwärme
- **Ammoniak** → `OEO_00010000` — Ammonia is a portion of matter with the chemical formula NH3. It has a gaseous normal state of matter and can act as an air pollutant. As it can be oxidised it can be used as a fuel.
- **Anthrazit** → `OEO_00000058` — Anthracite is a hard coal with a high caloric value due to its high carbon content (about 90 % fixed carbon).
- **B7** → `OEO_00010448` — B7 is a diesel fuel that consists of fossil diesel fuel and up to 7 % biodiesel and that is liquid.
- **Benzin** → `OEO_00000183` — Gasoline (American English) or petrol (British English) is a portion of matter in the form of a transparent petroleum-derived liquid that is used primarily as a fuel in spark-ignited internal combustion engines. It consists mostly of organic compounds obtained by the fractional distillation of petroleum, enhanced with a variety of additives.
- **Bio-Ottokraftstoff** → `OEO_00000075` — Biogasoline is a portion of matter that has a liquid state of matter and has a gasoline fuel role. It consists of bioethanol, biomethanol and products of these two substances. It is made from vegetable or animal material and thus has a biogenic origin.
- **Biobrennstoff** → `OEO_00000072` — A biofuel is a combustion fuel that has a biogenic origin.
- **Biodiesel** → `OEO_00000071` — Biodiesel is a portion of matter that has a liquid state of matter and has a diesel fuel role. It is produced from plants or animals and thus has a biogenic origin.
- **Bioethanol** → `OEO_00010446` — Bioethanol is ethanol with a biogenic origin.
- **Biogas** → `OEO_00000074` — Biogas: Durch anaerobe Vergärung entstehendes Gasgemisch aus hauptsächlich Methan und Kohlendioxid, das als Brennstoff dient.
  - also written: aufbereitetes Biogas, Klärgas, Faulgas, Klärschlammgas
- **Biomasse** → `OEO_00010214` — Biomasse: Stoff pflanzlichen oder tierischen Ursprungs und damit biogener Herkunft.
  - also written: pflanzliche Biomasse, landwirtschaftliche Biomasse
- **Biomethan** → `OEO_00010215` — Biomethan: Aus Biogas durch Abtrennung von Kohlendioxid gewonnenes Gasgemisch aus hauptsächlich Methan, das als Brennstoff dient.
- **Braunkohle** → `OEO_00000251` — Braunkohle: Nicht backende Kohle mit einem Brennwert unter 17 435 kJ/kg und über 31 % flüchtigen Bestandteilen.
- **Brennstoff** → `OEO_00000173` — A fuel is a portion of matter that has the disposition to be an energy carrier and that has a fuel role.
- **Brent-Rohöl** → `OEO_00010408` — Brent crude is a crude oil that is extracted in the North Sea and traded at ICE London.
- **Dampf** → `OEO_00110001` — Steam is water that has a gaseous state of matter.
- **Dieselkraftstoff** → `OEO_00010242` — A diesel fuel is a combustion fuel that has a diesel fuel role.
- **Druckgaskraftstoff** → `OEO_00320015` — A compressed gas fuel is a combustion fuel that has a compressed gas fuel role.
- **Druckluft** → `OEO_00000102` — Compressed air is air that has been compressed to store energy. To discharge energy, the air is expanded.
- **Düsenkraftstoff** → `OEO_00000245` — Jet fuel is kerosene used for aviation turbine power units. It has the same distillation characteristics between 150 °C and 300 °C (generally not above 250 °C) and flash point as kerosene. In addition, it has particular specifications (such as freezing point) which are established by the International Air Transport Association (IATA).
- **E10** → `OEO_00010447` — E10 is a gasoline fuel that consists of (fossil) gasoline and up to 10 % ethanol and that is liquid.
- **Elektrolysewasserstoff** → `OEO_00010379` — Electrolytic hydrogen is hydrogen that is a physical output of a water electrolysis process.
- **Elektrolysewasserstoff aus Kernenergie** → `OEO_00010416` — Nuclear electrolytic hydrogen is electrolytic hydrogen that is produced with nuclear electrical energy.
- **Elektrolysewasserstoff aus Solarstrom** → `OEO_00010418` — Solar electrolytic hydrogen is electrolytic hydrogen that is produced with solar electrical energy.
- **Endenergieträger** → `OEO_00140080` — A final energy carrier is an energy carrier that has the disposition final energy carrier disposition.
- **Erdgas** → `OEO_00000292` — Erdgas: Gasgemisch aus unterirdischen Lagerstätten, das hauptsächlich aus Methan besteht.
  - also written: Gas
- **Erdgas aus reinen Gasfeldern** → `OEO_00000297` — Non associated gas is natural gas originating from fields producing hydrocarbons only in gaseous form.
- **Erdölbegleitgas** → `OEO_00000062` — Associated gas is a natural gas that is a byproduct from crude oil exploitation.
- **Ethanol** → `OEO_00020001` — Ethanol is a portion of matter with the chemical formula C2H6O. It has a liquid normal state of matter and can be used as a combustion fuel.
- **Ethen** → `OEO_00020494` — Ethene, or colloquially Ethylene, is a hydrocarbon with the chemical formula H2C=CH2. It is gaseous in its normal state and can be used as a combustion fuel.
- **Fernwärme** → `OEO_00000132` — Fernwärme: Leitungsgebundene Wärmelieferung an Wohn- oder Gewerbegebäude.

- `label`: `refers to energy carrier`
- `outside_root`: `{'OEO_00000056': 'ambient heat', 'OEO_00000132': 'district heating', 'OEO_00000139': 'electrical energy', 'OEO_00000191': 'geothermal energy', 'OEO_00000388': 'solar thermal energy', 'OEO_00010114': 'waste heat', 'OEO_00140104': 'surface water heat', 'OEO_00140105': 'waste water heat', 'OEO_00310004': 'industrial waste heat'}`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `refers_to_energy_carrier`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is an OEO class (punning). The predicate is the MHPKG schema's slot, read from mhpkg_schema.yaml at serialization: refers to energy carrier. Its range is energy carrier, and the classes in outside_root are not under it in OEO; they are written all the same and counted, as input for the carrier axioms the ontology side is adding.

</details>

### `carrier_link_quote`

Only with window `base_year` or `target_year`: the passage where the row names that state ('carrier_raw'). 'carrier_quote' is then the frame's passage that prints the number.

### `carrier_link_source`

Which source 'carrier_link_quote' was found in.

### `carrier_means`

The entries of the document's list of names (its scenarios, its works) 'carrier' means: the entry its name is, or the entry of each member where its words name a group; empty where they name a group the field takes no members for, or where the request found they name no such thing (owner decision 2026-11-09). The graph writes the row to each of them, an empty list to none.

### `carrier_producer`

Who re-read 'carrier': the position of its entry in the `producers` list of the stamp beside the harvest. Written by a top-up for each coordinate it re-read, and by a pass that appended a parameter for each coordinate of the rows it wrote, never by the harvest, so a missing key means the harvest read it. A position the stamp has no entry for (the stamps were deleted) means nobody can say. Recorded and never compared.

### `carrier_quote`

The verbatim passage carrying 'carrier'. Present exactly when carrier_state is 'read'.

### `carrier_raw`

The document's own wording 'carrier' was read from. This is what a later re-mapping onto a changed vocabulary works on, so a choice without it is counted (raw_missing).

### `carrier_raw_foreign`

The wording does not name the option chosen for 'carrier': the model mapped a word the spec does not list onto this class. Kept and counted, not refused: some of those mappings are right.

### `carrier_seen`

A wording the model noticed for 'carrier' while answering 'not stated', or offered as an answer the closed list does not hold. Vocabulary review material, never evidence.

### `carrier_source`

Which source the passage for 'carrier' was found in. How far that may be from the row is the axis' own rule (own | local | any).

### `carrier_state`

How the coordinate 'carrier' ended. Always present: a missing key and a refused reading must not look alike.

### `carrier_window`

[stage, index] of the window 'carrier' was read in. `own`: at the value's own place, its row with its table's title and head or its sentence with the sentences beside it. `near`: in the pieces right before and after it. `found`: in the pieces a search found for the sentence of how the plan would print it. `piece`: a work read once for the value's piece, the line that names it or shows the piece is this publication's own. `entry`: the work of the entry of the document's list the value's name is. `plan_years`: a year none of these found, read from the plan's base and target years in a request of its own. The other stages are those of harvests made before the reading in pieces: `frame` is the one that is not a window: the coordinate was read ONCE for the document and applied to this row, and the index is which of the document's pairs it came from. `base_year`: the row's passage names the document's base state by word, and the index is the pair whose quote prints the number. `target_year`: the same for the document's target. `table`: read once for the row's table and taken by this row. `head`: the year the head of the row's column prints, one of the document's pairs, taken off the table and not asked; the index is the column. `retrieval`, `rest` and `places` are the windows of the reading by passages.

### `catalogue`

The open catalogue record a value was looked up in, not read off the page: its source and id, what was searched, what it holds and which of its values matched the document's. The quote is the read value it was looked up with; the flag catalogue goes with it.

### `compute`

The sandbox runs behind a computed value: {code, stdout, ok, error}, the last with the numbers it was worked out from (inputs) and what they make (expected).

### `computed`

The value came out of the sandbox, not off the page; the quote proves the inputs it was computed from.

### `drawn`

A figure's value only: whether its image only draws the number (a bar, a segment, an area, a point of a curve) rather than printing it, as the reading said. True sets the flag read_off.

### `flags`

Non-fatal verifier findings. mapped:<axis>:<wording>-><uri> is the model mapping a word the spec does not list; period:* says whether a bare amount was shown to be a yearly one; review:* is what a second reading of this value under a narrower window came to, and only review:disagree is a reason.

### `head`

The head of the table column the number stands in, as the plan prints it. Taken from the table, never asked. Absent where the number is no table cell or its column is not certain.

### `kind`

### `parameter`

Spec parameter: Emissionen.

The prompt's own wording:

> Um WELCHE Kennzahl handelt es sich bei dieser Zahl? Entscheide danach, was die Quelle über sie sagt: die Einheit, die Spalten- oder Zeilenbeschriftung und die Tabellen- oder Abschnittsüberschrift. Eine Angabe in t CO2 ist eine Emission, eine Angabe in MWh oder GWh ein Energieverbrauch. Zitiere die Stelle, aus der das hervorgeht. Eine Angabe in kW, MW oder GW ist eine Leistung. Eine Angabe in % ist ein Anteil. Eine Zahl von Gebäuden ist eine Gebäudeanzahl.

### `qualifier`

Axis 'qualifier' of Emissionen. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless qualifier_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Welches Wort direkt bei dieser Zahl sagt, wie genau sie gilt, etwa „ca.“, „bis zu“, „mehr als“ oder „zwischen“?

<details><summary>What it may answer (5)</summary>

- **höchstens** → `out:at_most` — Der Plan nennt die Zahl als Obergrenze: Der Wert ist höchstens so groß.
  - also written: bis zu, maximal, max., weniger als, unter
- **mindestens** → `out:at_least` — Der Plan nennt die Zahl als Untergrenze: Der Wert ist mindestens so groß.
  - also written: mehr als, über, ab, min.
- **oberes Ende einer Spanne** → `out:range_high` — Die größere von zwei Zahlen, zwischen denen der Wert liegt.
  - also written: bis
- **ungefähr** → `out:approximate` — Der Plan nennt die Zahl als ungefähren Wert.
  - also written: ca., circa, rund, etwa, knapp, annähernd
- **unteres Ende einer Spanne** → `out:range_low` — Die kleinere von zwei Zahlen, zwischen denen der Wert liegt.
  - also written: zwischen, von

</details>

### `quantity`

Axis 'quantity' of Emissionen. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless quantity_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Welche Größe ist diese Zahl?

<details><summary>What it may answer (11)</summary>

- **Anteil in Prozent** → `out:share` — Ein Anteil in Prozent, keine Menge.
  - also written: Anteil an den Gesamtemissionen, EE-Anteil, Anteil erneuerbarer Energien
- **CO2 emission value** → `OEO_00340066` — CO2-Emissionswert: Quantifiziert eine CO2-Emissionsrate mit Masseneinheit, nur Kohlendioxid, etwa in t CO2.
  - also written: CO2-Emissionen, Kohlendioxidemissionen, CO2-Ausstoß
- **Emission je Kopf oder je Einwohner** → `out:specific` — Ein Wert je Fläche, Kopf oder Gebäude, keine Gesamtmenge.
- **Emissionsfaktor** → `OEO_00340063` — An emission factor value is a fraction value that quantifies an emission factor as quotient of the emissions or removals of a gas per unit activity.
- **Emissionswert** → `OEO_00010079` — An emission value is a quantity value that quantifies an emission rate and has a mass unit.
- **Treibhausgasemissionswert** → `OEO_00340065` — A greenhouse gas emission value is an emission value that quantifies a greenhouse gas emission rate and has a mass unit.
- **abgeschiedene oder gespeicherte Emission** → `out:captured` — Eine abgeschiedene oder gespeicherte Menge, das Gegenteil einer ausgestoßenen.
  - also written: CO2-Abscheidung, CCS, CCU, Senke
- **carbon dioxide equivalent quantity value** → `OEO_00140083` — CO2-Äquivalent: Quantifiziert die kombinierte Wirkung aller Treibhausgase als CO2-Äquivalent, etwa in t CO2-Äq., auch wo eine Festlegung des Plans seine Emissionen in CO2-Äquivalenten angibt und die Kopfzeile nur CO2 nennt.
  - also written: Treibhausgasemissionen, THG-Emissionen, CO2-Äquivalente, CO2e-Emissionen, THG, CO2e, Treibhausgas
- **etwas anderes** → `out:other` — Etwas, das keiner der übrigen Einträge dieser Liste ist.
- **kumulierte Emission über mehrere Jahre** → `out:cumulative` — Über mehrere Jahre aufsummiert, keine Jahresmenge.
  - also written: kumulierte Emissionen, Emissionsbudget
- **vermiedene oder eingesparte Emission** → `out:avoided` — Eine vermiedene oder eingesparte Emission, das Gegenteil einer ausgestoßenen.
  - also written: THG-Minderung, THG-Einsparung, Emissionsreduktion

</details>

- `role`: `type`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

The class of the value node itself (rdf:type). An out:* answer is a deliberate non-class and mints no node.

</details>

### `scenario`

Axis 'scenario' of Emissionen. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless scenario_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Auf welchen Zustand des Plans bezieht sich diese Zahl, auf seinen Bestand, ein Trend- oder ein Zielszenario?

<details><summary>What it may answer (3)</summary>

- **Bestand** → `status_quo` — Erhoben oder bilanziert für ein vergangenes oder das laufende Jahr: Bestandsanalyse, Ist-Zustand, Bilanzjahr, Ausgangslage, Energiebilanz, „bislang“, „derzeit“, „wird verbraucht“, oder ein Jahr vor der Erstellung des Plans im Titel.
  - also written: Ist-Zustand, Bilanzjahr, Status quo, Bestandsanalyse, Basisjahr, Ist-Zustandsanalyse, Ist-Analyse, Erstellungsjahr
- **Trendszenario** → `trend` — Die Fortschreibung ohne zusätzliche Maßnahmen.
  - also written: Referenzszenario, Weiter-wie-bisher, Business as usual
- **Zielszenario** → `target` — Der angestrebte Zustand eines künftigen Jahres, wie der Plan ihn erreichen will, auch unter einem eigenen Namen des Plans („bei erhöhter Energieeinsparung“, „Umsetzungsszenario 2“, „Transformationspfad“).
  - also written: Zielbild, Zielwert, Zielpfad, Umsetzungsszenario, Transformationspfad

</details>

- `linked_by`: `{'label': 'has part', 'node': 'heatplan', 'schema_slot': 'has_part'}`
- `map`: `{'status_quo': {'schema_class': 'AggregatedInventoryAnalysis'}, 'target': {'schema_class': 'TargetScenario'}, 'trend': {'class': 'OEO_00020311', 'note': 'with existing measures scenario. Not OEO_00020314 reference scenario: that one is defined by a ROLE (`scenario and has role some reference role`) and carries no content, so it says a scenario was used as a comparison and not what it assumes. A Trendszenario is the continuation under the measures already decided, which is what WEM means.'}}`
- `role`: `parent`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Which container node holds the value: the plan has the container by `linked_by`, and the edge from the container down to the value is structural and named in kg.py. Only the target scenario is serialized today (G2).

### `sector`

Axis 'sector' of Emissionen. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless sector_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Für welchen Verbrauchssektor gilt diese Zahl, oder, wo ihre Tabelle oder ihr Satz nicht nach Sektoren aufteilt, wonach teilt die Stelle stattdessen auf?

<details><summary>What it may answer (17)</summary>

- **Abfall- und Abwassersektor** → `OEO_00010036` — A waste and wastewater sector is a sector that covers activities related the collection and treatment of waste and wastewater.
- **Energienachfragesektor** → `OEO_00000128` — An energy demand sector is a sector that covers energy consumers.
- **Energieumwandlungssektor** → `OEO_00000158` — An energy transformation sector is a sector that covers energy production, energy transformation from primary to secondary energy and transport of energy to the demand sector.
- **GHD** → `OEO_00000405` — Sektor für nicht-industrielle gewerbliche Aktivitäten.
  - also written: Gewerbe, Handel, Dienstleistungen, Gewerbe/Handel/Dienstleistungen, öffentliche Gebäude, öffentliche Liegenschaften, kommunale Einrichtungen, GHD/Kommune, Gewerbe, wirtschaftlich genutzte Gebäude, kommerzieller Sektor
- **Gebäudesektor** → `OEO_00010034` — A building sector is a sector that covers buildings.
- **Industrie** → `OEO_00000227` — Sektor für industrielle Aktivitäten mit Energiewandlung.
  - also written: Industriesektor, verarbeitendes Gewerbe
- **Landwirtschaft** → `OEO_00010035` — Sektor für Landwirtschaft, Forstwirtschaft und Landnutzung.
  - also written: Land- und Forstwirtschaft
- **Mineralölverarbeitung** → `OEO_00010319` — A mineral oil refining sector is an energy transformation sector that covers mineral oil refining.
- **Private Haushalte** → `OEO_00000214` — Sektor für private Haushalte und Wohngebäude.
  - also written: Haushalte, Privathaushalte, Wohnen, Wohngebäude, Haushaltssektor, Einfamilienhaus, Mehrfamilienhaus
- **Quellgruppe** → `OEO_00010360` — A source category is a sector that is defined by a common reporting format.
- **Sonstige** → `out:other` — Etwas, das keiner der übrigen Einträge dieser Liste ist.
  - also written: Andere
- **Stromsektor** → `OEO_00000145` — An electricity sector is a transformation sector that covers electricity generation and transport of electrical energy to consumers of electrical energy.
- **Summe** → `out:total` — Die Zahl gilt für alle Verbraucher zusammen: ihre Zeile oder Spalte summiert ausdrücklich über die Sektoren, sie heißt ausdrücklich Gesamtverbrauch oder Gesamtbedarf aller Verbraucher, oder ihre eigene Tabelle oder ihr Satz ist gar nicht nach Sektoren aufgeteilt, nur nach anderem (Energieträger, Gebiet). Ein Gebiet („Gesamtstadt“, „Stadtgebiet“) und die Gesamtleistung von Anlagen sind keine Summe über Sektoren.
  - also written: Gesamt, alle Sektoren, insgesamt, Gesamtverbrauch, Gesamtbedarf
- **Teilsektor** → `OEO_00010464` — A subsector is a sector that is a part of an other sector.
- **Verkehr** → `OEO_00000422` — Sektor für den Transport von Personen und Gütern.
  - also written: Verkehrssektor
- **Wärme- und Kältesektor** → `OEO_00000213` — A heating and cooling sector is a sector that covers heating and cooling activities.
- **internationaler Verkehr** → `OEO_00010320` — An international transport sector is a transport sector of international transport processes.

</details>

- `label`: `refers to sector`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `refers_to_sector`
- `total_class`: `OEO_00000128`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is an OEO sector class. The predicate is the MHPKG schema's slot, read from mhpkg_schema.yaml at serialization. A number its table does not split by sector (out:total) is written as total_class, the parent the schema names for a figure reported without a sector breakdown.

</details>

### `technology`

Axis 'technology' of Emissionen. A closed list; an entry beginning 'out:' is a deliberate non-class answer and mints no node. null unless technology_state is 'read' or 'derived'.

The prompt's own wording:

> Welcher Wärmeerzeugungstechnologie ordnet der Plan diese Zahl ausdrücklich zu, oder, wo ihre Tabelle oder ihr Satz nicht nach Technologien aufteilt, wonach teilt die Stelle stattdessen auf?

<details><summary>What it may answer (12)</summary>

- **Biomasseheizung** → `OEO_00020493` — Biomasseheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Biomasse.
  - also written: Holzheizung, Pelletheizung, Hackschnitzelheizung, Biomassekessel
- **Direktelektrische Heizung** → `OEO_00020487` — Direktelektrische Heizung: Wandelt elektrische Energie direkt in Wärme mittels elektrischer Heizelemente um.
  - also written: Stromdirektheizung, Elektroheizung, Nachtspeicherheizung
- **Gasheizung** → `OEO_00020491` — Gasheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Erdgas.
  - also written: Gaskessel, Gasbrennwertkessel, Gastherme
- **Geothermie** → `OEO_00020306` — Geothermie: Kombiniert geothermische Einheiten und Wärmeprozesse zur Wärmeerzeugung.
  - also written: Geothermieanlage, Erdwärmesonden, Erdwärmekollektoren
- **Kohleheizung** → `OEO_00020489` — A coal powered heating technology is a combustion-based heat generation technology that describes how to combine combustion-based heaters, heat gain processes, coal and energy in a specific way.
- **Solarthermie** → `OEO_00020307` — Solarthermie: Nutzt Solareinheiten und solare Umwandlungsprozesse zur Wärmeerzeugung.
  - also written: Solarthermieanlage, solarthermische Anlage
- **Sonstige** → `out:other` — Etwas, das keiner der übrigen Einträge dieser Liste ist.
  - also written: Andere, sonstige Wärmeerzeuger
- **Summe** → `out:total` — Die Zahl gilt für alle Wärmeerzeuger zusammen: ihre Zeile oder Spalte summiert ausdrücklich über die Technologien, oder ihre eigene Tabelle oder ihr Satz ist gar nicht nach Heiztechniken aufgeteilt, nur nach anderem. Ein Energieträger allein („Erdgas“) ist keine Technologie, eine „Gasheizung“ schon.
  - also written: Gesamt, insgesamt, alle Technologien
- **Verbrennungsbasierte Heizung** → `OEO_00020488` — A combustion based heating technology is a heat generation technology that describes how to combine combustion-based heaters, heat gain processes, combustion fuels and energy in a specific way.
- **Wasserstoffheizung** → `OEO_00020492` — Wasserstoffheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Wasserstoff.
  - also written: Wasserstoffkessel, H2-Kessel
- **Wärmepumpe** → `OEO_00020486` — Wärmepumpe: Kombiniert Wärmepumpen, Wärmegewinnungsprozesse und Energieträger zur Wärmeerzeugung.
  - also written: Wärmepumpen, Luft-Wasser-Wärmepumpe, Sole-Wasser-Wärmepumpe, Großwärmepumpe
- **Ölheizung** → `OEO_00020490` — Ölheizung: Verbrennungsbasierte Wärmeerzeugung unter Verwendung von Heizöl.
  - also written: Ölkessel, Heizölkessel

</details>

- `label`: `refers to technology`
- `role`: `edge`
- `schema_slot`: `refers_to_technology`

<details><summary>Why</summary>

Object is an OEO heat generation technology class (punning), the MHPKG schema's slot. A number not split by technology (out:total) writes no edge: the schema's slot is optional.

</details>

- **biogener Industrieabfallbrennstoff** → `OEO_00010225` — A biogenic industrial waste fuel is an industrial waste fuel that has a biogenic origin.

- **erneuerbarer Brennstoff** → `OEO_00000033` — A renewable fuel is a fuel that has a renewable origin and an renewable energy carrier disposition
- **erneuerbarer Elektrolysewasserstoff** → `OEO_00010380` — Renewable electrolytic hydrogen is electrolytic hydrogen that is produced with renewable electrical energy.
- **erneuerbarer Energieträger** → `OEO_00020050` — A renewable energy carrier is an energy carrier that has a renewable energy carrier disposition.
- **fester Brennstoff** → `OEO_00010144` — A solid combustion fuel is a combustion fuel that has a solid state of matter under normal conditions.
